# PrepPilot: Project Progress Report

**Project:** PrepPilot (QUIZBOT)  
**Problem statement:** PS 2 — Adaptive AI Study Companion  
**Report date:** 31 October 2026  
**Stage:** Working local prototype for hackathon early evaluation

## 1. Executive summary

PrepPilot is an adaptive study companion that helps learners choose what to practise,
respond to questions, understand mistakes and plan their next practice step. It combines
a Python learner model with a browser-based study interface and optional Gemini support.
The adaptive engine works independently of the language-model provider.

Work completed includes the initial quiz engine, verified-bank handling, setup and
account pages, a subject/chapter dashboard, a home hub, a progress page, a placement
check, user-supplied question-paper import, interactive study buddies, reporting,
focus blocks and session summaries. Reliability work addressed report isolation,
account-file concurrency, session resume, stale timers and placement/PDF regressions.

The latest local verification passed 18 API regression tests, 11 persistence and
access-control tests, 7 bookmark tests, offline AI-provider routing tests, engine
self-checks, checks covering 154 bank questions, and PDF extraction regression checks.
Learner progress, accounts, preferences, papers and bookmarks persist in SQLite, and
every learner resource is checked against the signed-in owner. The system is suitable
for a supervised local demonstration. Independent educational validation and public
deployment hardening remain unfinished.

## 2. Problem and objectives

A fixed quiz sequence treats learners alike even when their weaknesses and reasons
for mistakes differ. Correctness alone does not distinguish a rushed attempt, a
confident misconception, a time-pressure error or a possible conceptual gap.

PrepPilot's objectives are to:

1. Estimate understanding at chapter level and select relevant practice.
2. Use response evidence beyond right/wrong answers.
3. Explain why a question was chosen and what should happen next.
4. Support focused study with manageable practice blocks and breaks.
5. Allow practice on both supplied bank items and the learner's own papers.
6. Keep selected bank-backed practice usable without an AI key.

The project uses heuristics to infer possible learning needs; it cannot directly
observe a learner's thought process or diagnose fatigue clinically.

## 3. Current learner journey

1. **Entry:** Sign up, sign in or continue as a guest.
2. **Setup:** Select class/exam, subjects, starting difficulty, explanation style,
   timed mode and focus length. Available focus choices are 25, 40 and 60 minutes.
3. **Optional placement:** Complete a five-question check to estimate starting
   subject readiness. It attempts to spread questions across selected subjects.
4. **Home hub:** See continuation and recommended-next-practice cards, subject
   cards and saved personal paper entries.
5. **Chapter selection:** Select a subject, search its chapters and start a quiz.
6. **Practice:** Answer with confidence, request hints, skip or use timed mode.
   The engine updates the learner estimate and chooses follow-up practice.
7. **Review:** Inspect question-selection reasoning, progress, error patterns and
   a session summary with a recommended next step.
8. **Study rhythm:** The focus timer begins with the quiz. When the block ends,
   the current question can be finished before the server starts a break.

Reloading resumes an existing session while the same server is running. Chapter
switching now retains the learner model rather than resetting it. An active break
is carried across preference changes. Restarting the server still resets progress.

## 4. Features implemented

### 4.1 Adaptive learner model

The engine maintains chapter abilities, subject priors, attempt counts,
misconception flags and response history. An IRT-inspired Elo model estimates:

`P(correct) = 1 / (1 + exp(-(ability - question_difficulty)))`

Question selection balances chapter weakness, exploration, misconception signals
and recent repetition. Item difficulty targets roughly 60% estimated success when
suitable items exist. Explicit chapter focus constrains selection to that chapter.

Confidence, timing and hints affect learner updates. Answer switching contributes
to diagnosis and wellbeing signals. Very fast responses receive weaker evidence
weight; hinted correct answers receive reduced credit.

### 4.2 Feedback and learning support

The system labels possible error types: concept gap, careless slip, trap option
and time pressure. Depending on the pattern, it can request an easier follow-up,
switch topics or provide a confidence-building question.

The reasoning panel explains the selection in plain language, with technical
engine details available separately. Readiness, confidence calibration, simplified
negative-marking advice, predicted scores and highest-payoff chapters are shown
as estimates. They have not been calibrated against actual exam outcomes.

### 4.3 Study interface

The interface includes landing, login, setup, study hub, quiz, progress and paper-import
pages. The dashboard uses subject cards, chapter search and availability indicators.
The quiz result and reasoning panels were simplified to reduce clutter.
Desktop and mobile flows have received focused browser checks, including a
375-pixel layout; a complete accessibility audit has not been performed.

### 4.4 Class and exam support

Syllabus maps cover Classes 1–12, JEE Main, JEE Advanced and NEET. JEE Main was
added as a separate level with its own target difficulty and +4/−1 marking model.
JEE Advanced uses a simplified +3/−1 model and NEET uses +4/−1. These settings do
not model all possible section-specific exam rules.

Syllabus breadth exceeds question-bank coverage. Some levels and chapters require
AI generation; curriculum alignment still needs review against authoritative material.

### 4.5 Question bank and optional AI

The checked-in bank has 154 items across 46 distinct chapter labels:

| Category | Items | Current check |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Original numeric questions | 54 | Recomputed answers matched against options |
| Original conceptual questions | 26 | Keys matched to stored reviewed answers |
| Imported JEE Advanced questions | 74 | Source-provided keys retained; structure checked |

Items failing the bank checker are excluded at startup. Passing these checks does
not guarantee educational correctness, particularly for conceptual and imported
items that are not independently solved by the checker.

Gemini optionally supplies generated questions, personalized explanations and
answers to doubts. Generated questions must agree with a second solve before
being accepted. Background prefetching, fallback models and rate-limit cooldown
handling are implemented. Two model calls can share errors, so agreement is not
a correctness guarantee. Provider availability, latency and quotas remain dependencies.

### 4.6 User-supplied question papers

Learners can paste typed questions, CSV content or a Google Sheets link. The browser
can extract text from PDF, DOCX and PPTX files before import. The importer recognises
question/option structures and answer-key sections, validates entries and reports
skipped questions. Uploaded papers are saved locally and appear as selectable quizzes.
Their quiz pools remain restricted to the uploaded questions rather than adding
AI-generated questions.

Uploaded answer keys are supplied by the user, not independently certified. Scanned
PDFs and image-only slides are unsupported without OCR. Complex reading order and
unusual layouts may still need manual correction. Legacy DOC/PPT files require
conversion to DOCX/PPTX.

### 4.7 Accounts and study buddies

Accounts use salted PBKDF2-SHA256 password hashes stored in SQLite; legacy
`users.json` accounts are imported unchanged. Sign-in issues a random token that is
stored only as a SHA-256 digest with an expiry and sent as an HttpOnly cookie. API
middleware identifies the owner of every request, rejects cross-origin writes, and
sessions, papers and bookmarks are only visible to their owner. Bookmarks can only be
saved after a question is answered or skipped, because they include the answer.

Three interactive painted study buddies—Pip, Mika and Leo—provide answer reactions,
encouragement and click interactions. Their visual assets are served locally.
Reduced-motion styles are present. Leo's sad asset currently duplicates his idle
image, as documented in the handoff notes.

## 5. Architecture and data

| Component | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| FastAPI / `main.py` | Routes, sessions, accounts, Gemini integration and study rhythm |
| `engine.py` | Learner updates, selection, diagnosis, strategy estimates and simulation |
| `syllabus.py` | Subject/chapter maps and exam levels |
| `papers.py` | Paper parsing, validation, saving and loading |
| HTML/CSS/JavaScript | Setup, dashboard, quiz, progress, imports and study-buddy interactions |
| Bank JSON files | Original questions and imported source-key items |

The frontend uses vanilla JavaScript, KaTeX and Chart.js. Fonts and some libraries
load from external CDNs. Runtime dependencies are pinned to FastAPI 0.162.0 and
Uvicorn 0.54.0; regression tests use httpx.

State storage currently works as follows:

- **SQLite (`preppilot.sqlite3`, `storage.py`):** accounts, hashed login tokens,
  preferences, learner sessions (JSON, never pickle), question papers and bookmarks.
- **Server memory:** a cache of active learners and AI-generated questions.
- **Browser storage:** display caches only; the server cookie is the identity.

The supported operating model is one server worker, because active learners are
cached in process.

## 6. Reliability fixes completed

| Issue | Resulting behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Reports deleted shared bank questions | Questions are hidden only for the reporting session; shared bank content is retained |
| Older report buttons targeted the latest answer | Reports carry exact answer IDs and operate on the matching response |
| Incomplete report rollback | Retained answer evidence is replayed to rebuild learner state and predicted-score history |
| Concurrent sign-ups could overwrite accounts | A lock protects read/modify/write operations and atomic replacement prevents partial files |
| Browser reload discarded progress | Matching browser sessions resume while the server remains running |
| Old browser timers caused immediate breaks | New sessions ignore stale browser clock values |
| Extra focus-time overlay duplicated setup | The original setup page owns preferences; the dashboard handles chapter selection |
| Chapter changes reset learning | Existing learner sessions are reused and only the focus changes |
| Placement could resume an ordinary session | Explicit placement requests start a placement-enabled session; active checks remain resumable |
| Reporting distorted placement estimates | Replay preserves placement-specific subject updates and restores missing assessment slots |
| PDF fragments merged adjacent options | Fragment boundaries retain whitespace while explicit line endings are preserved |

Placement/PDF fixes were implemented after new tests reproduced the earlier failures.
Their checks then passed with the changes applied.

## 7. Testing and verification

The latest local validation completed successfully:

- **18 API regression tests:** reporting isolation and replay, account concurrency,
  session resume, stale clocks, focus duration, chapter selection, break preservation,
  JEE Main, paper import, placement and hub/summary behavior.
- **Engine self-check:** ability direction, subject-prior effects, response weighting,
  diagnosis, wellbeing, strategy and synthetic adaptive comparisons.
- **Bank checker:** all 154 stored items pass their applicable checks.
- **PDF JavaScript regression checks:** the real extraction function is exercised
  with PDF.js-style fragmented options, wrapped text and page boundaries.
- **Targeted parser check:** corrected inline options are accepted by the paper parser.
- **Browser checks across development:** setup-to-chapter practice, timer timing,
  resume, mobile overflow, and normal-practice-to-placement transition. The latest
  placement browser check also confirmed that reloading resumes the assessment.

GitHub Actions is configured for Python checks and the PDF JavaScript check. A
successful local run is not a claim that a particular remote GitHub workflow has
executed. PDF fragment tests do not replace testing diverse real documents.

## 8. Simulation results and interpretation

The engine self-check averages 10 synthetic runs per profile:

| Artificial learner profile | Adaptive questions | Static questions | Reported reduction |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Uneven abilities | 235 | 421 | 44% |
| Weak all-round | 312 | 422 | 26% |
| Nearly exam-ready | 140 | 177 | 21% |

The simulator assumes learning gain peaks when difficulty is close to true ability.
The adaptive policy aims near ability, while the static comparator cycles chapters
with a broad difficulty distribution. This demonstrates behavior under a constructed
model; it does not establish improved outcomes in real learners. The UI uses a
20-run comparison, so displayed averages may differ from this self-check table.

## 9. Documentation and repository preparation

Work completed includes a root README, developer notes, architecture and roadmap,
evaluator/demo guide, submission description and abstract, contribution/security
notes, third-party attribution, issue/PR templates and CI configuration.

Earlier handoff work also produced clean upload packaging and a combined runnable
Python distribution with a README and description. Those were snapshots at the
time of creation; they must be regenerated from current source before submission
if they do not include later features and fixes.

The project remains unlicensed at the owner's request. JEEBench's upstream notice
is preserved for imported material. Private accounts, API keys, installed environments
and build-session transcripts must not be included in a public submission. Some
older documentation retains earlier test counts or milestone descriptions; this
report describes the current reviewed working tree, including uncommitted fixes.

## 10. Limitations and unfinished work

1. One server worker only: active learners are cached in process. Expired tokens and
   old guest data are not yet cleaned up, and there are no rate limits.
2. Question papers saved as files before the SQLite migration are not imported.
3. Some chapters depend on Gemini or Groq and can be affected by quota, latency or model availability.
4. Original conceptual keys, imported mappings and AI output need educator review.
5. No real learner study has established learning gains, diagnosis accuracy or score calibration.
6. PDF extraction is text-based; OCR and general layout recovery are not implemented.
7. Comprehensive accessibility, security and browser regression coverage is incomplete.
8. Prerequisite graphs, spaced repetition, exam-date planning and peer benchmarking
   have not been implemented.

## 11. Recommended next phase

**First:** strengthen verified content in a focused set of demo chapters and obtain
teacher review. **Second:** test short sessions with a small learner group and record
usability observations without overstating effectiveness. **Third:** automate the
main browser journey and test failure recovery for AI and document import.

A hackathon demonstration should emphasize the complete adaptive loop: placement,
chapter selection, answer evidence, visible reasoning, follow-up and actionable
session summary. The interface and buddies support that story; the learner model
and trustworthy feedback are the central contribution.

## 12. Conclusion

PrepPilot has progressed from an initial adaptive quiz implementation into an
integrated local learning prototype with setup, content selection, personal papers,
progress views and study support. Its core flows have targeted automated evidence,
and several important regressions have been reproduced and corrected. The next
milestone is to strengthen persistence, access control and educational validation
while keeping the demonstration focused and reliable.

# Architecture

## Components

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Browser[Browser: setup, study space, quiz] --> API[FastAPI: main.py]
    API --> Syllabus[syllabus.py]
    API --> Engine[engine.py: learner and policy]
    API --> Bank[Verified bank JSON]
    API --> Sessions[In-memory learner sessions]
    API --> Accounts[Local users.json: password hashes]
    API --> Gemini[Optional Gemini API]
```

There is no frontend build step. FastAPI serves HTML pages and JSON endpoints.
The browser uses vanilla JavaScript, localStorage, KaTeX and Chart.js. `engine.py`
is independent of web and provider calls.

## Learner journey

1. `/login` creates an account or a distinct guest identity.
2. `/setup` saves class/exam, subjects, initial ability, focus length and preferences
   to browser storage. This does not create a quiz session.
3. `/study` fetches `/api/catalog`, filters configured subjects, and presents chapters
   with available bank counts or AI requirements. Browsing does not start the timer.
4. Start quiz opens `/app?new=1&focus=...`. The app creates a session, applies focus
   through `/api/focus`, then requests `/api/next`.
5. `/api/answer` updates the model; `/api/dashboard` exposes learner estimates.
6. Reloads use the saved session ID when settings match. A new chapter launch
   intentionally creates a fresh session. Learning is not merged across sessions.

## Adaptive loop

`Learner` stores chapter abilities, counts, flags, subject priors and answer history.
Selection uses weakness + exploration + misconception boost − recent-repeat penalty.
The item selector seeks an unseen item near the desired difficulty, with bank/AI
fallbacks. Correctness, confidence, timing and hints weight ability updates;
answer switching additionally informs diagnosis and wellbeing heuristics.

Question difficulty calibration for verified items is shared in memory. Predicted
scores use a simplified equal chapter and difficulty-band model, not a calibrated
exam prediction. Focus blocks are enforced by the backend; the browser renders
countdowns and finishes the current question before requesting the next.

## Reports and concurrency

Answer IDs bind reports to exact responses, including old result cards. Reporting
records the question locally to that session, reverses its item adjustment and
replays retained answer evidence to rebuild chapter state and predicted history.
Shared bank entries are not deleted. Same-session quiz mutations are locked.

Sign-up uses salted PBKDF2-SHA256 with 200,000 iterations. An in-process lock
serializes account creation and atomic replacement prevents partially written
JSON reads. This is single-worker protection, not a multi-process database.

## State and external services

| State | Location | Lifetime |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Account profiles/password hashes | `preppilot/users.json` | Local file; excluded from repository |
| Preferences and session pointer | Browser localStorage | Until cleared; browser-specific |
| Progress, reports, tokens, generated cache | Server memory | Until server restart |
| Original/imported bank | Checked-in JSON | Repository data; runtime difficulty drift is in memory |

Gemini is called server-side using environment credentials. Generation, explanations
and doubts may transmit question content, selected answers or learner-entered text
to Google. Fonts and math/chart libraries load from external CDNs. The application
does not provide user-data deletion controls, a full privacy policy or a production
security boundary yet. See SECURITY.md.

# PrepPilot Project Guide

What the project does and how it works

31 October 2026

PrepPilot is the adaptive study companion built in the QUIZBOT project for PS 2 Adaptive AI Study Companion. It is a working local hackathon prototype for students practising school subjects and entrance examination topics. This guide explains the problem, the product journey, the implemented features, the adaptive method, the architecture, the verification evidence and the work needed next.

The central result is a complete practice loop: choose a learning context, select a chapter, answer with confidence, see feedback and selection reasoning, and receive a follow-up suited to the current learner estimate. Accounts, guest identities, preferences, sessions, papers, bookmarks and mistake reviews now have durable local storage. Optional Gemini and Groq services extend content and tutoring; the Python engine chooses the learning policy.

This is an early evaluation product. Readiness, predicted scores, mistake categories and wellbeing signals are heuristic estimates. The synthetic simulation is a demonstration of the policy under stated assumptions. No real learner study establishes better exam results, diagnostic accuracy or clinically meaningful emotion detection.

The guide is intended for project owners, teammates, hackathon evaluators and developers continuing the work. It describes the current implementation and includes the current interface refinement: a galaxy-led dashboard, simpler entry flow and a more deliberate typography hierarchy.

## Guide contents

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 1 Project purpose and product decisions

### The problem

A fixed question list treats students with different starting knowledge as if they need the same practice. It also treats different mistakes alike. A learner who rushes, a learner who confidently follows a misconception and a learner who needs a basic explanation can all give the same wrong answer. A useful companion should use additional response evidence to choose what happens next and make that choice understandable.

PrepPilot combines chapter-level ability estimates with confidence, time, hints and option switching. It aims to keep practice challenging enough to be useful while giving the learner a visible reason for the next question. Focus blocks, revision tools and tutoring support the larger study session around that loop.

### Intended audience and scope

The syllabus supports Classes 1 to 12, JEE Main, JEE Advanced and NEET. Primary levels use Maths, English and EVS; middle and secondary levels include Maths, Science, Social Science and English; senior levels use Physics, Chemistry, Maths and Biology as appropriate. Exam maps filter subjects and chapters for their target context.

Syllabus coverage is much broader than bank coverage. The 154 checked-in questions apply only to selected chapters within Class 11, Class 12 and the exam levels. Younger classes and uncovered chapters require an AI provider. A chapter in the syllabus is not a promise that a bank-backed quiz exists for it. Curriculum labels and mappings still need independent review against current authoritative materials.

### What was built and why

- A pure Python learner engine makes selection explainable and testable without an AI call.
- A subject and chapter dashboard lets learners choose a concrete starting point before practice.
- A local question bank gives a repeatable demonstration that works without paid or available AI.
- Gemini and Groq add generated questions, explanations and tutoring where appropriate.
- SQLite storage gives accounts durable, private progress rather than depending on browser names or session IDs.
- Bookmarks and scheduled mistake review turn completed questions into revision material.
- Personal paper and note imports let learners bring material relevant to their own course.
- A knowledge galaxy visualizes the same syllabus and learner model that power the ordinary chapter list.

The contribution is the integration of these parts into a visible response-to-follow-up loop. IRT-inspired ability estimation, Elo-style updates and spaced review are established ideas; the project does not claim to have invented them.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 2 Website walkthrough

### First visit and returning visit

The landing page at `/` and the sign-in route at `/login` serve the same entry page with a sign-in panel. A learner can create a password account, sign in, use configured Google entry, or continue as a guest. Successful entry restores server-owned preferences. A first-time learner goes to setup; a returning identity with saved preferences goes to Home at `/study`. An existing guest cookie can restore its guest preferences instead of starting over. Already signed-in accounts opening the entry page also return to the appropriate destination.

The seven-step setup wizard asks for a class or exam, subjects, starting difficulty or a placement check, focus duration, explanation style, timed mode and AI provider. Normal setup choices are 25, 40 or 60 minutes. Just starting, comfortable and confident seed the model at approximately -1, 0 and +1. Explanation styles are step by step, short and analogy based. Settings are saved on the server. Preferences in the profile menu reopens the same editor.

The current interface refinement makes the study dashboard the first destination after saving setup. Learners who select placement can launch it from the dashboard before chapter practice. The five-question placement mechanism itself remains part of the quiz engine. This keeps an explicit dashboard step between onboarding and practice.

### Home and chapter practice

Home presents the knowledge galaxy, subject and chapter browsing, continuation and next-practice actions, review availability and personal papers. An optional three-step guest tour explains the study space, chapter choice and other study tools; it can be dismissed. Selecting a subject narrows the chapter list; search helps locate a chapter. Availability reflects whether the selected provider or bank can supply questions. The galaxy offers a visual route to the same chapters.

Starting a quiz opens `/app` with the requested chapter focus. Focus timing belongs to the practice session, rather than time spent reading the dashboard. Choosing another chapter within a compatible session retains the learner model and ongoing rhythm. Returning to the quiz can restore an active question, hint count and focus or break countdown.

### Answer feedback and follow-up

The learner chooses one of four options and a confidence level, can request hints, and submits. Feedback reveals the correct answer and reference explanation, any heuristic mistake diagnosis, and a visible model update. An optional AI explanation can address a wrong choice. The next item is chosen from the allowed scope using current ability, available content and any follow-up or confidence-builder instruction.

After answering or skipping, Bookmark becomes available. End session shows attempts, accuracy, time, readiness changes, the main error pattern and a recommended next chapter. Progress gives deeper model views; Mistake review revisits scheduled misses. Breaks pause question delivery until the server countdown finishes.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 3 Detailed feature catalog

### Personalized learning context

The level selector distinguishes school classes, JEE Main, JEE Advanced and NEET. Subject selection limits the syllabus considered by a session. Starting difficulty seeds subject priors so a new learner does not always begin at the same estimate. A five-question placement check rotates across selected subjects when suitable questions are available and applies stronger subject-prior updates than ordinary chapter practice.

Placement is a short starting estimate, not a standardized assessment. It is resumable, and a specific placement request does not silently resume an ordinary quiz. Reporting a placement answer rebuilds the remaining evidence and restores the missing assessment slot. Changing material setup preferences can create a new learner session; an active break carries over.

### Adaptive chapter and item selection

The engine maintains ability, attempt count and misconception flags per chapter, plus subject priors for unseen chapters. It ranks eligible chapters by weakness, exploration and misconception signals, with a recent-repeat penalty. It then selects an unseen item close to the desired difficulty. Explicit chapter practice restricts selection to that chapter; subject practice narrows the candidate set to that subject.

The normal target is roughly a 60 percent estimated chance of success when an appropriate item exists. Content scarcity, an easier follow-up or a confidence-building intervention can change the actual item chosen. A small bank can be exhausted. The app should explain unavailable content rather than imply that every chapter has unlimited verified questions.

### Response evidence and mistake feedback

Correctness drives the ability update. Very fast responses receive weaker evidence weight; correct answers with hints receive less credit; slow correct answers provide weaker evidence of fluent mastery. Confidently wrong responses receive a stronger update and a misconception flag. Option switching is retained for diagnosis and wellbeing patterns.

Wrong answers receive one of four possible labels: concept gap, careless slip, trap option or time pressure. The label selects a suggested fix. Concept gaps and traps can trigger an easier follow-up in the same chapter; slips and time pressure use different feedback. These labels describe response-pattern rules, not direct knowledge of what the learner was thinking.

### Explainable selection and live adaptation

The quiz exposes why a question was chosen, including chapter-selection signals and estimated success probability. The live adaptation chart shows chapter skill estimates alongside item difficulty, with answer outcomes and a short explanation of the latest change. More detailed history remains on Progress. This gives a learner a way to inspect the policy instead of receiving unexplained difficulty changes.

<!-- pagebreak -->

### Study rhythm and timed practice

Focus blocks use the selected duration. A normal break is one quarter of the focus block; a longer break is three eighths when the heuristic wellbeing state calls for it. For a 40-minute block, these are 10 and 15 minutes. The focus period can finish while a question is open; the learner can complete that question before the next-item route locks for a break.

The server enforces breaks with HTTP 423 responses. Reloading, changing a chapter, changing setup or restarting the server does not erase a saved active break. A new block does not immediately break before any answered practice. A long absence of at least about 10 minutes can count as rest and begin a fresh focus block while keeping learning history. A manual early-break action is also available.

Timed mode adds per-question countdowns and automatically submits at zero. The current simplified timing is 150 seconds for JEE modes, 60 seconds for NEET and 90 seconds for school classes. These are practice settings rather than a full replica of every exam section. Demo mode at `/app?demo=1` uses a two-minute focus block and 30 or 45 second breaks.

### Wellbeing rules and confidence building

Three consecutive misses trigger a frustrated state. A tired state requires at least two patterns such as a large accuracy drop, slower responses, repeated skips or frequent option changes, with sufficient history. The app can offer an early break, easier content or a strong-chapter confidence builder. Interventions are spaced to avoid repeating a message on every answer.

These are lightweight engagement rules. They do not read facial expressions, voice, physiology or clinical mental state. Chapter constraints and content availability can limit the proposed change of topic.

### Progress and examination strategy

The Progress page collects predicted-score trends, attempts, chapter and subject readiness, error patterns, confidence versus observed accuracy, and highest-payoff chapter suggestions. Confidence calibration groups answers by sure, unsure and guessing, then compares their accuracy with expected marks under the chosen marking model.

JEE Advanced uses a simplified +3/-1 single-correct model; JEE Main and NEET use +4/-1. School practice defaults to no negative marking. The model can illustrate when skipping has positive expected value. It does not model changing section rules, real paper weightings or every official scoring scheme.

Chapter percentages are withheld until a small minimum of evidence is available, and score estimates require more total answers. Existing UI thresholds of three chapter answers and five total answers improve presentation; they do not establish statistical calibration. Recommended-next chapters estimate which small ability gain would add the most modeled marks, rather than promising a measured return on study time.

<!-- pagebreak -->

### Private bookmarks for revision

A learner can save a question after submitting or skipping it. The server rejects bookmarking the current unanswered question because a saved snapshot includes the answer. It also rejects unseen questions and question IDs that do not belong to the learner's session. Saving the same question twice is deduplicated.

The Bookmarks page at `/bookmarks` supports search, answer and explanation review, and removal. SQLite stores a question snapshot, so a saved question survives a restart or its source paper being archived. Account bookmarks return after sign-in; guest bookmarks remain associated with the guest cookie. Bookmarking is a revision convenience, not secure graded-exam delivery.

### Scheduled mistake review

Missed questions enter the learner's private review queue. The initial due time is one day later. Correct review answers move the item to three days, then seven days; a third correct review completes the ladder and removes it from active review. A miss restarts the one-day stage. Demo sessions use minutes instead of days.

The `/review` page provides a mini quiz. The API withholds answers and explanations until a review is answered and rejects attempts before the due time. Home shows due counts or the next scheduled review. Reporting a question removes it from review. Review answers do not update the current adaptive learner model because the item may belong to a different class, exam or paper.

### User supplied question papers

The `/papers` page accepts pasted questions, CSV, a supported Google Sheets link, or text extracted from PDF, DOCX, PPTX and text files in the browser. It recognizes separate or inline options and answer-key sections. Answer labels can use A to D, 1 to 4 or exact option text. Optional metadata includes chapter, subject, difficulty, explanation and hints.

CSV and Sheets imports require question, four options and answer columns. A template is available at `/api/papers/template.csv`. The parser validates four distinct options and a resolvable answer. Invalid entries are skipped with an explanation. Limits include 500 questions and a two-megabyte import bound. Sheets must allow readable CSV export; the server constructs a Google export URL from the accepted sheet identifier.

Imported papers are owned SQLite records and appear as their own practice level, `paper:<id>`. Their chapter structure and difficulty feed the normal learner engine, but the question pool stays restricted to that paper. AI does not silently add new questions to it. Removing a paper from the active list archives it and retains related saved practice. Old unowned paper JSON files require re-import.

Scanned PDFs, image-only slides and handwritten pages require OCR, which is not built. Old DOC and PPT files must be converted. Complex layout or multi-column reading order may need manual correction. User-supplied answer keys are not certified by importing them.

<!-- pagebreak -->

### AI providers and adaptive content

Setup and quiz options offer Gemini, Groq and Bank only. Choices without a configured server key are unavailable. With no saved choice, the code prefers configured Groq, then Gemini, then Bank only. Provider configuration is not a live health check of credentials, quota or model access.

Question generation, second-solve checking, personalized explanations and doubts follow the selected provider. Provider-specific generated caches, pending prefetch jobs and cooldowns prevent a choice from mixing with another provider's work. Switching providers preserves the learner, current question and break. The app does not silently send a failed request to the other vendor. Gemini can retry its configured fallback model within Gemini.

Bank-backed practice remains available without either provider. For uncovered chapters, provider latency, quota or invalid output can leave no usable question. The UI explains AI unavailability rather than claiming guaranteed content. Groq uses standard-library HTTP requests and JSON-object mode; no extra provider SDK is required.

### Tutor on notes and general questions

The `/tutor` page can explain concepts, answer questions and create quizzes from pasted or uploaded notes. PDF, Word, PowerPoint and text extraction happens in the browser using shared extraction code. Notes are saved in browser local storage. A requested tutoring action sends the relevant text to the selected server-side AI service.

Explain concepts returns a short summary and up to ten concept explanations in the saved explanation style. The prompt asks the provider to correct mistakes gently. Ask a question uses notes when there is at least a paragraph of material; without sufficient notes it becomes a general tutor at the learner's configured class or exam. The last six chat turns can be sent as context. Prompts fence notes as study material and tell the model to ignore embedded instructions; this is a mitigation, not a guarantee against prompt manipulation.

Quiz me offers 5, 10 or 15 MCQs in the interface. A separate solve checks the generated answer keys. Items that fail validation or agreement are dropped, so the final quiz may contain fewer questions. Survivors become an owned personal paper labeled AI-generated from notes, with `key_source` set to `ai-notes` and `verified` false.

Notes requests use at most 30,000 characters of notes. Explanations and note quizzes require a paragraph of at least 80 characters; general questions can work without notes. Long-document retrieval, chunking and OCR are outside the current implementation. The saved notes are not synchronized as account documents across devices.

<!-- pagebreak -->

### Reporting and AI checks for disputed keys

An ordinary question report targets an exact answer ID, including an older result card. The app hides that question for the reporting session and replays retained answers to rebuild ability, subject priors and predicted-score history. Repeating the report is safe. Shared bank content remains available to others. Reports persist with the learner session.

The `/check` page adds an optional AI-assisted check of recently answered questions. It lists up to 15 distinct eligible questions from the learner's latest session and accepts an optional concern. The first solve does not receive the stored key as the answer to copy. If it disagrees, a second solve checks the item again. Only matching disagreement, including matching no-correct-option results, triggers the private hide-and-replay path. All attempts at that question in the checked session are removed from learning evidence.

An unclear result leaves the key in place. Malformed AI answers do not remove a question. Official-key disagreements display a caution, and completed checks enter a private complaints log. Two calls can share a model's error, so the AI verdict is assistance for review, not an authoritative correction of an exam key.

### Accounts and Google entry

Password accounts have a name, normalized email, selected level and salted password hash. Guest entry issues a separate server-owned identity. Server preferences determine first-time setup and returning Home behavior for password, Google and guest entry. The account menu links study pages, Preferences and sign-out; guests can create an account or leave the guest session.

Optional Google entry uses Google Identity Services with a configured Web client ID. The backend asks Google's tokeninfo endpoint to verify the token and checks audience, issuer, expiry, verified email and subject. A verified Google email can enter an existing password account with that email. Google-only accounts do not gain an invented password login.

The local Google project is configured for `http://localhost:8000` and remains in external Testing mode. The Google-rendered button has been seen locally. A completed real Google login has not been exercised. Offline tests validate token-claim handling and returning-account restoration; other test accounts or tunnel origins require Google project configuration.

### Study buddies and installable app

Pip, Mika and Leo are local painted characters that react to answers and clicks. Learners can switch, hide, drag or reposition a buddy with the keyboard; browser storage remembers display choices. Reduced-motion support stops animation. Leo's sad asset currently duplicates the idle pose. The character artwork was created with Canva's AI image generator and prepared locally.

The PWA manifest supplies icons and study shortcuts. Install prompts depend on browser support and a secure context such as localhost or HTTPS. The service worker caches pages and static assets; `/api/` is never cached. Previously visited pages may open offline, but accounts, saved progress updates, answers and AI requests still require the running server. Installation does not provide a complete offline quiz engine.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 4 Adaptive method and interpretation

### Ability update

For chapter ability theta and question difficulty b, estimated correctness is `P(correct) = 1 / (1 + exp(-(theta - b)))`. The update is `theta = theta + K * weight * (score - P)`. K decreases with chapter attempts but retains a floor so the estimate can keep tracking change within a session.

An ordinary correct response starts with score 1; hints lower that credit by 0.3 each, to a minimum of zero. Incorrect responses score zero. Confidence sets an evidence weight, with additional rules for very fast, slow or confidently wrong answers. Every response nudges the subject prior by 30 percent of the chapter change. Placement adds the remaining 70 percent to give the subject stronger initial evidence.

Unseen chapters inherit their subject estimate. This lets evidence inform more than the single attempted chapter, while also introducing an assumption: strength in one chapter may not transfer to every chapter in the subject. Readiness is the model's probability at a chosen exam or class target difficulty. It is not percentage syllabus completion.

### Policy and intervention

Chapter priority combines `1 - readiness`, an exploration bonus that falls with attempts, a capped misconception bonus and a penalty for one of the two most recent chapters. A small random term breaks ties. The difficulty target is ability minus the logit of 0.6, with an additional reduction for easier follow-ups. Selection picks the closest available unseen item. Verified items receive small in-memory difficulty adjustments from answers; this shared calibration is not a durable or externally calibrated item bank.

Correctness, response weighting, diagnosis, wellbeing and strategy each have distinct roles. Option switching affects diagnosis and wellbeing rather than directly entering the basic evidence function. A follow-up instruction can alter chapter or difficulty choice. User-selected focus still bounds the available scope.

### Score estimates and synthetic evidence

Predicted score averages expected marks across chapters and four difficulty bands around the exam target. It compares answering everything with answering only positive expected-value questions. Highest-payoff chapters compare the modeled marks before and after a small hypothetical ability increase. Neither calculation uses validated real-paper weighting or a trained outcome predictor.

The documented 10-run engine self-check reports the following synthetic comparison. The UI uses 20 runs, so its displayed averages can differ.

| Artificial learner profile | Adaptive questions | Static questions | Reduction |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Uneven abilities | 235 | 421 | 44 percent |
| Weak all round | 312 | 422 | 26 percent |
| Nearly exam ready | 140 | 177 | 21 percent |

The simulator defines learning gain to peak near true ability. The adaptive policy aims near ability; the static policy cycles chapters with a broad difficulty distribution. This constructed result supports a mechanism demonstration. It is not observed student improvement, clinical evidence or proof of an exam-score benefit.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 5 Question content and answer trust

### Bank composition

The checked-in bank contains 154 single-correct MCQs across 46 distinct chapter labels. Not every label belongs to every level, and not every syllabus chapter has an item. The bank combines 80 original questions with 74 imported JEE Advanced questions.

| Source and check category | Count | What the check establishes |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Original numeric items | 54 | A stored computation matches exactly one option and the key |
| Original conceptual items | 26 | The key matches stored reviewed option text |
| Imported official-key items | 74 | Source-key provenance is retained and the record structure passes checks |
| Total | 154 | Each item passes its applicable repository check |

The original items use common examination concepts and patterns with original wording or values. The 26 conceptual answers were reviewed with AI assistance and still need educator review. A stored review is less evidence than an independent solution. Imported items are not all re-solved by the checker. The word verified in application data means the repository check passed, not that an educator has certified the question.

### Imported JEE Advanced material

`import_jeebench.py` transforms JEEBench material from JEE Advanced 2016 to 2023 and retains official-key provenance. The importer includes 74 supported single-correct MCQs from the candidate subset and skips formats that do not parse cleanly, including tables. Multi-correct, integer and other unsupported answer types are outside the current quiz format. Chapter mapping can use Gemini or a keyword fallback and needs review.

The upstream JEEBench MIT license and copyright notice are retained in `docs/JEEBENCH_LICENSE.txt`. Reference: Daman Arora, Himanshu Singh and Mausam, Have LLMs Advanced Enough? A Challenging Problem Solving Benchmark For Large Language Models, EMNLP 2023. The repository notice records that an independent rights audit of the original exam material has not been performed.

### Startup checking and AI agreement

The server runs the bank checker at startup and excludes failing IDs. Adding an original question requires a corresponding computed or reviewed entry in `verify_bank.py`. This prevents unchecked additions from silently becoming bank practice.

AI-generated adaptive questions use a second solve and are accepted only on answer agreement. Note-generated quizzes apply structural checks and a separate batch solve. Uploaded papers retain user-provided keys. These are different trust categories and should keep their visible source labels. A second AI call is independent in prompt or solve, not necessarily in vendor, model or error pattern. Agreement reduces some mistakes but cannot certify correctness.

Educator review, clearer explanations, source-map checks and expanded bank coverage are the highest-value content improvements. Do not present all 154 items as independently machine-solved; only the 54 numeric originals have that specific check.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 6 Architecture and data flows

### Components

| Component | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| main.py | FastAPI pages and APIs, identity, sessions, selection orchestration, AI transport and rhythm |
| engine.py | Pure learner policy, updates, diagnosis, wellbeing, strategy and simulation |
| storage.py | SQLite accounts, tokens, preferences, sessions, papers, bookmarks, reviews and complaints |
| syllabus.py | Class and exam subject and chapter maps |
| papers.py | Pasted paper and CSV parsing, answer matching, validation and Sheets export handling |
| HTML pages and site.css | Entry, setup, dashboard, quiz, progress, revision, papers, tutor and checks |
| account.js and google-signin.js | Server-state restoration, navigation, profile menu and optional Google button |
| extract.js and galaxy.js | Browser document extraction and the syllabus visualization |
| pwa.js and sw.js | Installation and controlled caching |

The frontend uses vanilla JavaScript. FastAPI 0.162.0 and Uvicorn 0.54.0 are the pinned runtime dependencies. httpx 0.28.1 supports API tests. Browser libraries include KaTeX, Chart.js, Three.js, PDF.js, mammoth and JSZip where needed. Fonts and several libraries load from external services.

### The practice data flow

1. Entry creates or restores a server-owned identity with an authentication cookie.
2. The browser reads `/api/state` and saves setup through `/api/preferences`.
3. The dashboard reads level, chapter availability, continuation and recommendation data.
4. Starting practice creates or resumes an owned session with scope and rhythm settings.
5. The engine ranks chapters and chooses target difficulty; orchestration selects bank, uploaded or accepted generated content.
6. The next-question response withholds the answer. Submission records evidence and returns feedback.
7. The session is serialized as JSON into SQLite; a miss can also schedule a review.
8. Summary, Progress, galaxy and recommendations read the resulting learner state.

SQLite is authoritative. The process caches hydrated learner objects, generated questions, background work and shared item calibration. Per-session locks serialize mutations. Use one server worker because those caches and locks are process-local. Scaling to multiple workers requires deliberate ownership and concurrency work.

Current SQLite schema version 4 contains users, auth_tokens, preferences, study_sessions, question_papers, bookmarks, reviews and complaints. Legacy account import preserves hashes and is idempotent. Old unowned paper files are retained but not exposed as owned records. JSON serialization avoids unsafe pickle loading. Short reloads retain active questions and hints; account login can restore preferences and progress from another browser.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 7 Security and privacy

### Implemented protections

Passwords use salted PBKDF2-SHA256 hashes. Random authentication tokens have a 30-day expiry and are stored as SHA-256 digests. Cookies are HttpOnly and SameSite=Lax. Logout revokes the current token. `PREPPILOT_SECURE_COOKIES=1` enables Secure cookies for HTTPS operation; local HTTP uses development-compatible cookies.

API middleware identifies the request owner and requires authentication or a guest identity for learner routes. Database and cache access check ownership for sessions, papers, bookmarks, reviews and other private records. Browser names, preferences and session IDs alone do not establish identity. Mutation requests with a mismatched Origin header are rejected. A small set of entry, catalog, provider, simulation and template routes is public.

Question and AI text should be inserted with `textContent`, then formatted with KaTeX, rather than injected as HTML. Keys remain on the server, and public provider configuration exposes availability without secrets. Sheets handling accepts recognized Google URL patterns and builds the export request rather than offering arbitrary URL fetching.

### Where information goes

| Information | Current handling |
| --- | --- |
| Account and learning records | Local SQLite database and its journal files |
| Authentication secret | Browser HttpOnly cookie; only its digest is stored server side |
| Notes | Browser local storage; requested text goes to the selected AI provider |
| Imported files | Browser extracts text; imported questions are saved as owned papers |
| AI prompts | May include question context, choices, explanations, doubts, notes and recent tutor turns |
| Display preferences | Some buddy, theme and interface choices remain browser local |
| External assets | Browser may contact font, CDN and Google identity services |

Guest data belongs to the issued guest identity. Clearing or losing its cookie can remove access to that progress. Creating a separate account is not a documented guest-to-account migration. Account login supports cross-browser recovery of account-owned records; it does not synchronize browser-only notes or every visual preference.

### Operational boundaries

This is not a production security certification. Rate limiting, account recovery, administrative tools, expiry cleanup, data retention and deletion policies, consent design and a broad audit remain unfinished. Public catalog and simulation APIs are not an abuse-control system. Student data and keys must not enter a public repository or submission package.

Keep `.env.local`, `preppilot.sqlite3`, WAL/SHM files, legacy accounts, old private papers, transcripts and local environments out of shared source. Use the documented SQLite backup process rather than copying a live database without considering its journal. The service worker deliberately excludes API caching, but normal browser-local notes and cached pages still require consideration on shared devices.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 8 Interface and accessibility

### Design direction

The interface uses a navy and amber observatory identity, with a warm paper alternative in light mode. The current refinement gives main titles Playfair Display, subheadings Fraunces, and body and utility text DM Sans with sans-serif fallbacks. This hierarchy separates the main task from support information. Typography and visual styling do not alter the learner policy or question evidence.

The dashboard emphasizes the galaxy and direct subject-to-chapter practice. Secondary controls sit in menus, and a small optional guide helps first-time learners understand the dashboard. Returning users retain a direct route to continuation and Preferences through the profile menu. The intention is to make a first useful study action visible without requiring the learner to read every model detail.

### Knowledge galaxy

The current Three.js galaxy represents subjects as planets. In overview, chapter arcs appear in particle belts around each subject; focusing a system reveals chapter moons. Readiness drives red-to-amber-to-green color; unassessed chapters have a pale treatment; misconception flags add a pulse. Interaction supports rotation, zoom, chapter inspection and a practice action.

The landing page includes a separate sample study report with a scroll-led galaxy journey. Home uses the learner's real data in its own scroll-led subject journey. Scroll position focuses subject systems and their reports. Sample progress on the landing page is labeled as a demonstration. Learner report metrics show zero practice and unassessed states when evidence is absent rather than inventing progress.

The galaxy visualizes estimates rather than measured knowledge itself. A chapter that inherits a subject prior is not independently assessed. Normal chapter cards and the list fallback remain necessary for precise navigation and accessibility. The visualization lazily starts when visible and pauses off-screen or in a hidden tab to limit unnecessary work.

### Current access support and gaps

The product includes a keyboard-reachable galaxy list, reduced-motion behavior, responsive layouts, profile-menu keyboard controls and buddy keyboard movement. The galaxy uses the flat list when WebGL is unavailable or reduced motion is requested. Focused browser checks have covered desktop and a 375-pixel viewport, light and dark modes, reduced motion and answer-only bookmarking. Earlier contrast fixes addressed subject icons and bright glows.

This evidence is targeted. It does not establish complete screen-reader support, WCAG conformance, every browser/device combination or a full accessibility audit. All newly edited flows still require a current browser pass. Responsive styles do not by themselves prove a page works at every narrow width.

The PWA makes entry convenient, while its offline page explains the need to reconnect. Interface caching must not be confused with offline learner processing. Source changes can require a service-worker cache version update so installed browsers fetch revised entry and navigation code.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 9 Installation and operation

### Local setup

Use Python 3.12 or newer, a modern browser and an internet connection for package installation. From the QUIZBOT root, create an isolated environment and install the application dependencies:

```powershell
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
python -m pip install -r preppilot/requirements.txt
cd preppilot
python start_local.py
```

Open `http://localhost:8000`. For a bank-only demonstration, continue as a guest, choose JEE Advanced, select Bank only and Comfortable, save setup, then choose Maths and Quadratic Equations on Home. Some suitable content is available without an AI key. Read each item for more than three seconds if demonstrating ordinary evidence weight.

On macOS or Linux, activate the environment with `source .venv/bin/activate`. The equivalent direct server command from `preppilot` is `python -m uvicorn main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000`. Direct Uvicorn reads the process environment; the convenience launcher additionally reads the root private settings file. Run a single worker.

### Optional private configuration

Use `.env.example` as a list of supported names. Save real values only in the Git-ignored root `.env.local`, then start with `python start_local.py`. The launcher reads Gemini and Groq keys and model names plus the Google client ID. This example contains placeholders only:

```text
GROQ_API_KEY=your_private_key
GROQ_MODEL=llama-3.3-70b-versatile
GEMINI_API_KEY=your_optional_private_key
GEMINI_MODEL=your_accessible_model_id
GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=your_public_web_client_id
```

The code's current Gemini default is `gemini-3.5-flash`, followed by `gemini-flash-lite-latest`; the Groq default is shown above. These are repository defaults, not a guarantee that an account has access. Bank only needs no AI key. The launcher accepts `--host` and `--port`; its defaults bind locally on port 8000. Set Secure-cookie configuration in the process environment for an HTTPS deployment, since the launcher's private-file allowlist does not include it.

Google entry needs a Web OAuth client with every used origin configured. The current localhost client remains in Testing mode. Different hostnames, ports and changing tunnel URLs are distinct origins. No client secret is needed for the implemented Google ID-token flow. Restart after changing Python or environment configuration; refresh after ordinary frontend edits and account for PWA caches.

### Persistence and recovery

The database is created under `preppilot` when the app starts. Back it up using `docs/PERSISTENCE_MIGRATION.md`. Legacy account migration leaves the original file unchanged; older memory-only sessions cannot be recovered. Do not rerun the historical scripts in `pending`; the bundle is already integrated and those scripts are not idempotent.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 10 Testing and evidence

### Automated verification

The latest completed verification has 75 Python tests plus three Node checks, along with engine and bank self-checks. The suite breakdown below describes the current test inventory. Provider and Google tests use synthetic inputs and mocked responses; they do not spend live quota or prove live OAuth configuration.

| Suite | Tests | Principal coverage |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| test_regressions.py | 21 | Replay, placement, scope, rhythm, papers, resume, JEE Main and PWA files |
| test_ai_providers.py | 7 | Routing, transport, cooldown, cache separation and provider switching |
| test_persistence.py | 11 | Restart, ownership, authentication, migration, archives and long absence |
| test_bookmarks.py | 7 | Answer-only saves, exact snapshots, deduplication and private access |
| test_reviews.py | 6 | Scheduling, mastery ladder, reporting removal and privacy |
| test_notes.py | 8 | Notes limits, tutor behavior, generated papers and second-solve filtering |
| test_account_extras.py | 15 | Google claims, returning preferences and disputed-key checks |
| Total Python tests | 75 | Local baseline rather than a remote CI result |

`test_pdf_import.js` exercises real extraction logic with fragmented PDF text, option spacing and page boundaries. `test_signin.js` checks first-time versus returning navigation, password and Google entry, server preferences, stale browser data and restoration errors. `test_setup.js` checks the wizard and dashboard-first setup behavior. The engine self-check exercises policy behavior and simulation. The bank checker passes all 154 applicable checks.

### Reproduce the checks

Install development dependencies, then run from `preppilot`:

```text
python -m pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
python -B engine.py
python -B verify_bank.py
python -B test_regressions.py
python -B test_ai_providers.py
python -B test_persistence.py
python -B test_bookmarks.py
python -B test_reviews.py
python -B test_notes.py
python -B test_account_extras.py
node test_pdf_import.js
node test_signin.js
node test_setup.js
```

GitHub Actions includes the established prototype checks; the current local verification also includes the new setup check. A remote CI pass is not claimed. Current browser checks confirm dashboard-first guest setup creates no quiz session, placement starts only on its explicit dashboard action, a 375-pixel phone layout has no horizontal overflow, returning guest preferences restore, the requested fonts are computed, and both galaxy reports show expected counts. A full automated end-to-end browser suite is still future work.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 11 Development history and reports

### How the project evolved

The initial system established the adaptive engine, syllabus maps, bank checking, optional generated content, feedback and focus rhythm. The study dashboard added a subject/chapter entry point, personal papers and an end-session summary. Later reliability work fixed exact-answer reporting, evidence replay, stale timers, placement restoration and PDF option boundaries.

The next phase added Groq provider selection and provider-specific caching, then permanent SQLite state and authenticated ownership. Revision grew through private bookmarks and spaced mistake review. The visual study space gained the galaxy and animated buddies. The PWA added installation and limited offline page access.

The integrated pending bundle added a shared account menu, optional Google entry, AI checks for disputed keys and general tutoring without notes. Follow-up fixes rejected malformed Google claims and AI answer values, handled flawed questions, replayed every attempt at a rejected question and improved responsive menus. Returning sign-in now uses server preferences to decide whether setup is needed. Current interface work simplifies onboarding and the dashboard while updating typography.

### Report directory

| Report or instruction | Purpose and use |
| --- | --- |
| README.md | Public project overview, run instructions and current capability summaries |
| preppilot/README.md | Extensive feature descriptions and accumulated implementation notes |
| AGENTS.md | Contributor rules, architecture constraints and required checks |
| HANDOFF.md | Chronological continuity record, completed work and known limits |
| PROJECT_REPORT.md | Earlier consolidated project and evaluation report |
| docs/ARCHITECTURE.md | Component, adaptive-policy and data-design background |
| docs/EVALUATION.md | Judge demonstration and early-evaluation guidance |
| docs/ROADMAP.md | Proposed improvements, including milestones subsequently completed |
| docs/PERSISTENCE_MIGRATION.md | Storage migration, backup and rollback procedure |
| docs/SUBMISSION.md | Reusable hackathon abstract and repository copy from an earlier milestone |
| SECURITY.md and CONTRIBUTING.md | Security status, private-data guidance and development conventions |
| docs/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md | Attribution and upstream licensing information |
| docs/PROJECT_GUIDE.md and .docx | Current consolidated project explanation and detailed feature catalog |

Older milestone text can describe memory-only progress, unauthenticated APIs, eight or eighteen tests, Gemini-only content, file-owned papers or spaced repetition as future work. Those statements are superseded by the current source. Existing submission text and screenshots should be refreshed before public reuse. Earlier archives and combined runnable snapshots should be regenerated from the current tree so they include later features and fixes.

This guide preserves the distinction between historical reports, implemented behavior and remaining work. The current app source and the latest completed verification should resolve conflicts between milestone descriptions.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 12 Limitations and roadmap

### Current limitations

- Question coverage is narrow compared with the syllabus. Uncovered chapters depend on a selected AI provider and may be unavailable during quota or network failure.
- Computed checks, stored reviews, official provenance and AI agreement establish different kinds of evidence. None replaces educator review of all content.
- Ability, readiness, error diagnoses and wellbeing rules are heuristics. No real learner evaluation or outcome calibration has been completed.
- Exam marking and paper difficulty are simplified. There is no complete official exam simulator, rank predictor or certified score forecast.
- Sessions and ownership persist, but process-local caches require one worker. Shared item-difficulty adjustments and generated caches are not durable calibration data.
- Guest access depends on its cookie. Account recovery, guest conversion, retention/deletion controls, rate limits and administration remain unfinished.
- Notes are browser-local and requests use a character cap. OCR, long-document retrieval and robust complex-layout recovery are absent.
- The PWA can open cached pages but needs the running server for quiz APIs and progress. It is not a standalone offline tutor.
- Google entry is configured locally in Testing mode; the real completed sign-in flow and other origins remain to be exercised.
- Accessibility, broad device coverage, deployment security and automated browser testing are incomplete.

### Recommended next phase

First, strengthen a small set of demonstration chapters. Have an educator independently solve and review conceptual items, imported mappings, generated questions and explanations. Record source category and review status clearly. Expand verified coverage only when the answers and chapter labels are dependable.

Second, run short learner sessions with consent and agreed measures. Observe whether students understand the dashboard, confidence input, reasoning and revision. Define outcome and comparison criteria before claiming educational gains. Recalibrate difficulty, readiness and score displays from evidence rather than from the simulation alone.

Third, automate the core browser journey and failure recovery: first entry, saved setup, chapter launch, answer, bookmark, report, break, restart and returning sign-in. Add provider-timeout and document-import cases. Audit keyboard, screen-reader and reduced-motion paths, including the galaxy list.

Before a public service, implement deployment controls: HTTPS configuration, rate limits, backup and restore drills, recovery, retention/deletion policy, student-data consent and a wider security review. Design multi-worker state coordination before increasing the worker count.

### Longer term ideas

Prerequisite knowledge graphs, goal and exam-date planning, more complete exam rules, teacher workflows and peer benchmarking remain ideas. Mistake-based spaced repetition is already implemented; a broader personalized scheduling system is still a possible extension. Community features, subscriptions and institutional dashboards are outside the current scope.

<!-- pagebreak -->

## 13 Demonstration and contribution guide

### A five minute bank backed demonstration

1. Start locally and continue as a guest. Choose JEE Advanced, Comfortable, Bank only, untimed practice and a normal focus duration. Save setup and arrive on Home.
2. Show the galaxy and ordinary chapter list. Open Maths, find Quadratic Equations and start practice. Explain that the timer belongs to practice rather than dashboard browsing.
3. Read the item, choose an option and confidence, then submit. Show the answer, explanation and model change. The under-three-second rule can weaken evidence, so avoid instant clicking if demonstrating normal learning updates.
4. Inspect Why this question and the live graph. A deliberately confident wrong answer can illustrate a possible misconception flag and easier follow-up, while acknowledging that the app is inferring from behavior.
5. Bookmark an answered item, show the summary, reload to demonstrate recovery and open Progress. If a miss exists, show its scheduled review. Use demo mode separately for the shorter break cycle.

Keep optional AI and Google demonstrations separate from the repeatable bank route. Only show them when the relevant local configuration and provider access work. If explaining synthetic savings, say the numbers come from artificial learners and a learning rule designed around difficulty matching.

### Questions evaluators may ask

Where does adaptation happen? In `engine.py`, with FastAPI selecting and serving available items. The AI provider supplies content rather than deciding the whole policy.

What makes feedback trustworthy? The project separates bank computation, reviewed keys, official provenance, uploaded keys and AI agreement, and offers reporting with evidence replay. Remaining uncertainty is visible and educator review is still needed.

Does progress persist? Yes. Account and guest state is saved in local SQLite. Account login can restore state in another browser; guest recovery depends on the original cookie. A public hosting or cloud synchronization service is not implied.

What is the project contribution? A complete, inspectable practice workflow joining response evidence, chapter selection, difficulty, support interventions, revision and optional tutoring.

### Contributing safely

Follow AGENTS.md, keep `engine.py` pure and add meaningful policy checks. Original bank additions need verification entries; imports need honest source labels and attribution. Use safe text rendering, preserve both color schemes and narrow-screen operation, and check browser changes in a real browser. Do not rerun the historical pending integration scripts.

No project-wide license has been selected for the original code. Public source visibility does not itself grant reuse rights. JEEBench and other upstream components retain their own licenses and notices. Discuss nontrivial contributions with maintainers. Before submission, add the actual team identity, hackathon name and verified repository/demo links; these are not invented in the guide.

### Terms used in the guide

Ability is the internal chapter estimate theta, not a measured exam grade.

Difficulty is item parameter b on the same scale; practice adjustments remain in memory.

Readiness estimates correctness at the target difficulty, not syllabus completion.

Subject prior supplies an unseen chapter's starting estimate; placement updates it more strongly.

Evidence replay rebuilds the model and history from retained answers after reporting.

Owned session binds progress to an account or guest; its identifier alone grants no access.

Second solve checks AI answer agreement; shared model errors remain possible.

PWA means progressive web app; installation and page caching still require online learner APIs.

### External attribution references

JEEBench source: https://github.com/dair-iitd/jeebench

Paper: https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.468/

See `docs/JEEBENCH_LICENSE.txt` and `docs/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` for the preserved upstream notice and credits.

# Early-evaluation guide

## What to evaluate

PrepPilot addresses PS 2: Adaptive AI Study Companion. The current milestone is a
working local prototype, with an explainable adaptive quiz loop and a subject/chapter
study dashboard. This is an early evaluation, not a production-readiness claim.

## Five-minute demo

Start the server using the root README. No Gemini key is needed for this route.

| Time | Action | Evidence to highlight |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0:00–0:45 | Continue as guest; select JEE Advanced, Comfortable, 25-minute focus and untimed mode | Preferences seed the learner and session |
| 0:45–1:15 | Open Maths, search Quadratic, click Start quiz | Chapter selection applies before the first item; timer starts here |
| 1:15–2:00 | Read a question, select an option and confidence, submit | Readiness update, timing and feedback |
| 2:00–3:00 | Make a confident wrong attempt; inspect the error diagnosis and next question | The heuristic follow-up and target difficulty; avoid claiming it knows the learner's true thought process |
| 3:00–4:00 | Open the reasoning and learner dashboard tabs | Why this question, chapter readiness, history and calibration |
| 4:00–5:00 | Reload to show resume; optionally report an answer or take an early break | Same-process resume, report isolation and server break enforcement |

Read the question for at least three seconds before submitting: faster attempts are
weighted as guesses. Exact question order and readiness changes can vary.
For a short focus/break demonstration, open `/app?demo=1` after setup; this uses a
2-minute block and 30/45-second breaks. Demo mode is separate from normal timing.
Do not wait 25 minutes during the main presentation.

## Evidence available

- Engine: `python -B engine.py` asserts key learner-update, diagnosis, wellbeing,
  strategy and synthetic comparison behavior.
- Bank: `python -B verify_bank.py` checks all 154 entries: 54 recomputed numeric,
  26 stored conceptual reviews and 74 imported source-provided keys.
- API: `python -B test_regressions.py` runs 8 tests covering reporting/replay,
  report isolation, concurrent sign-ups, reload resume, stale clocks, custom block
  timing, catalog validation and chapter-specific quiz launch.
- Manual automated-browser runs covered the guest flow, dashboard filtering,
  chapter launch, timer behavior and reload resume. Desktop screenshots and a
  mobile screenshot are in `docs/assets/`.
- CI is configured to run the Python checks once uploaded. No CI pass on GitHub
  is claimed before that workflow actually runs there.

## Simulation results and boundaries

The engine self-check averages 10 simulated runs for each profile:

| Artificial profile | Adaptive questions | Static questions | Reported reduction |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Uneven (2 strong, 4 weak) | 235 | 421 | 44% |
| Weak all-round | 312 | 422 | 26% |
| Nearly exam-ready | 140 | 177 | 21% |

The simulation defines learning gain to peak when difficulty is close to true
ability. The adaptive policy targets nearby difficulty; the static comparator
cycles chapters with uniformly sampled difficulty. It stops when all artificial
chapters meet the readiness threshold, or at the maximum question budget.
These assumptions favor the intended mechanism and must be disclosed. The
results are a sanity check, not causal evidence of student learning gains.
The UI comparison uses 20 runs, so its averages need not match this table exactly.

## Suggested judge questions

**Where is the adaptation?** `engine.py`, rather than a prompt. It estimates ability,
ranks chapters and targets question difficulty. Gemini is optional content support.

**How are answers checked?** By numeric recomputation or stored reviewed/source keys
for bank items, and a second solve for generated items. This is not a guarantee.

**Does progress persist?** Across reloads while the same server is running, through
a browser-stored session ID. Not across server restarts or devices.

**What is novel here?** The integrated response-evidence loop and visible reasons
for selection are the project contribution. IRT/Elo methods themselves are established.

**What comes next?** Durable account-linked storage, API authorization, educator
review, better coverage and real learner evaluation. See ROADMAP.md.

## Submission fields still owned by the team

Add the actual hackathon name, team/member names, GitHub URL, demo video URL and
any required slides in the submission portal. None of those identities or URLs
has been invented in these files.
