# SQLite progress migration and rollback

## Forward path

Stop the old server, then start the updated app with one worker. The local launcher
`python start_local.py` still loads the private provider configuration.

On startup, `storage.Database` creates the base schema and applies the additive version 2 bookmarks table in `preppilot/preppilot.sqlite3`.
It imports legacy `users.json` entries transactionally using insert-if-absent. Existing
SQLite accounts are not overwritten, and the JSON source is not changed or deleted.
If a legacy entry is malformed, import fails and its transaction rolls back; correct
that source from a backup before retrying. No malformed account is silently dropped.

New accounts, authentication tokens, preferences, learner-session JSON and owned papers
are written to SQLite. A process cache hydrates learners from JSON; no pickle is used.
Session mutations save before their response completes, including state changes that
return HTTP 423 for a break. Schema creation and legacy import can be retried.

Existing memory-only sessions cannot be recovered when the old process stops. Old
auth tokens require a new sign-in. Existing account passwords continue to work.

## Legacy papers and preferences

Legacy paper files have no trustworthy owner metadata. They remain under papers/ but
are not available through the owned API. Re-import to attach a paper to an authenticated
account or guest. The source files remain untouched. Browser-only setup preferences
from the old version must be selected once again; subsequent choices are saved on
the server. No learner is assigned another browser's old preference/paper cache.

## Identity and guest behavior

Sign-in/signup and Continue as guest set an HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookie. The database
stores only token digests and 30-day expiry. Session IDs are resource identifiers, not
authentication. Ownership is checked even for objects already in the process cache.
Guest data also persists, but is recoverable only while its browser cookie remains
valid. Account login can recover owned data from another browser.

## Backups

Keep provider credentials, legacy JSON files, paper sources and SQLite out of Git.
For a simple filesystem backup, stop the server first, then copy the database and any
remaining `-wal`/`-shm` files together into a private backup location. Do not copy only
the main database while the app is actively writing. SQLite's backup API is an
alternative for a properly coordinated live backup.

## Rollback

Stop the new server. Preserve the database and sidecar files as a private backup.
Restore the previous source version and use the retained original JSON files. The
old version cannot read newer SQLite progress, preferences, accounts or papers;
retain the database so those records can be recovered by the updated version or an
explicit export. Do not overwrite or delete it. Do not run old/new servers against
the same account data concurrently; a mixed-version rollout is not supported.

## Verification

- 18 existing API tests and 7 provider tests now run with authenticated guest clients
  and isolated temporary databases.
- 11 persistence/security tests cover reopen recovery, accounts, preferences, papers,
  cross-owner rejection, logout, expiry, migration retry/rollback, archived papers,
  cross-origin mutations and long-absence focus reset.
- A browser run stopped and restarted a separate server process and recovered the
  active quiz. A second browser login restored preferences and owned paper entries.

Production hardening and multi-worker learner-cache coordination remain separate work.

## Additional tables through 2026-10-31

Schema v3 adds owner-scoped spaced mistake reviews; v4 adds the `complaints` log
for AI question checks. Both use additive `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` migrations;
existing sessions, accounts, bookmarks and papers are preserved. Google-only account
profiles have a Google subject ID and no password hash; password login rejects them
and Google sign-in establishes the same existing HttpOnly cookie identity.

# PrepPilot: pitch facts sheet

Source material for the pitch deck. Everything here matches the app as built (October 2026). Figures under *Market* are approximate public numbers: check them against official NTA / CBSE releases before presenting, or drop them. Competitor rows are our reading of their public products: verify on their sites.

## 1. Product
- **Name:** PrepPilot, an adaptive AI study companion. Hackathon problem statement **PS 2: Adaptive AI Study Companion**.
- **One-liner:** Practice that adapts to every answer, and explains why it chose each question.
- **Covers:** Class 1–12 (CBSE/NCERT-aligned), JEE Main, JEE Advanced, NEET. Physics, Chemistry, Maths, Biology and school subjects.
- **Runs as:** a website and an installable app (PWA) on Android, iPhone and desktop, with an offline fallback page.

## 2. How it adapts (the core)
- **Skill per chapter:** each chapter has its own skill estimate (item-response model with Elo-style updates).
- **Question difficulty:** each question is set so the learner has about a **60% chance** of being right: hard enough to learn, not a guess.
- **Choosing the next chapter:** it weighs weakness, exploration of unseen chapters, and past misconceptions.
- **Evidence beyond right/wrong:**
  - confidence (sure / unsure / guessing);
  - time taken;
  - hints used;
  - switching away from the right answer.
- **Error diagnosis:** concept gap, careless slip, trap option, or time pressure, each with a specific fix.
- **"Why this question":** shown for every question.
- **Live adaptation graph:** after every answer it shows skill vs question difficulty, with an "↑ harder / ↓ easier" chip.
- **Simulation result:** reaches the readiness target with **21–44% fewer questions** than a fixed question bank, across 3 learner profiles (uneven, weak all-round, nearly exam-ready).

## 3. Learning features
- **Placement check:** 5 questions to set the starting level per subject.
- **Spaced mistake review:** missed questions return after **1, 3 and 7 days**; three right in a row means mastered; a miss restarts the ladder.
- **Daily goals and streaks**, plus an **exam countdown plan** ("N days left · Today: 20 min <chapter>, 10 min review").
- **Bookmarks** (after answering) and a **progress dashboard**: readiness per subject and chapter, mistake patterns, predicted score.
- **Knowledge galaxy:** a 3D map where subjects are planets and chapters orbit them, coloured red → amber → green by readiness. A list view is available.

## 4. Wellbeing
- **Focus blocks and breaks:** 40-minute focus blocks, then a **mandatory 10–15 minute break**, enforced by the server.
- **Fatigue and frustration detection:** triggers a "confidence builder" question or a break suggestion.
- **Study buddies:** painted mascots (Pip, Mika, Leo) react to answers with encouragement. They can be dragged anywhere and hidden.

## 5. Exam strategy
- **Confidence vs accuracy calibration:** when to trust your gut.
- **Negative-marking advice:** JEE Advanced +3/−1, JEE Main +4/−1, NEET +4/−1, including the break-even accuracy for attempting a question.
- **Live predicted score** and **highest-payoff chapters** to study next.

## 6. AI tutor and your own material
- **Ask anything:** a general tutor at the learner's level.
- **Learn from your notes:** upload notes (PDF, Word, PowerPoint, text) to get key concepts explained, ask questions about them, or **turn them into a quiz** (every AI question must survive a second, independent solve).
- **Bring your own question paper:** paste it, upload PDF/Word/PowerPoint, or link a Google Sheet, and it becomes an adaptive quiz.
- **AI question check:** a learner who disputes an answer key gets two independent AI solves; if both agree the key is wrong, the question is removed and their progress recalculated. Every check is logged.
- **Providers:** AI runs on **Groq by default, with Gemini as fallback**. The app **works fully without AI** (verified bank only).

## 7. Trust and quality
- **154 verified questions:**
  - **74 real JEE Advanced** questions with official answer keys (JEEBench dataset, credited, MIT licence);
  - 80 original past-paper-pattern questions;
  - every key is machine-checked or reviewed.
- **AI-generated questions** are tagged and second-solve checked; learners can report wrong ones.
- **Security:** password hashing (PBKDF2), secure HttpOnly cookies, per-learner data isolation, cross-site request checks, rate limits.
- **Quality:** over 60 automated tests (API, persistence, bookmarks, reviews, AI routing, notes tutor, sign-in, PDF import).
- **Tech stack:** FastAPI (Python), SQLite, vanilla JavaScript, Three.js, Chart.js, KaTeX; installable PWA.

## 8. What competitors don't have

| | Embibe | Physics Wallah / Unacademy | MARKS / ExamSide | Khanmigo / Duolingo | **PrepPilot** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Adaptive question selection | Yes | Mostly fixed tests | No | Partly | **Yes, per chapter** |
| Shows *why* each question was picked | No | No | No | No | **Yes, live** |
| Negative-marking strategy coaching | Partly | No | No | No | **Yes** |
| Fatigue detection and enforced breaks | No | No | No | No | **Yes** |
| Upload your own papers or notes | No | No | No | Partly | **Yes** |
| AI check of disputed answer keys | No | No | No | No | **Yes** |
| Spaced review of mistakes | Partly | No | No | Yes | **Yes** |
| Works without AI or a subscription | No | No | Yes | No | **Yes** |
| Large content library and video lessons | **Yes** | **Yes** | **Yes** | **Yes** | No (pilot) |

**Positioning:** big platforms adapt behind a curtain. PrepPilot adapts in the open, and treats students like people.

## 9. Real-world applications
- **Self-study:** for board exams, JEE and NEET, with an exam-date plan.
- **Coaching centres:** homework and revision, by uploading their own question sheets.
- **Schools:** revision weeks, plus spaced review so topics don't fade before exams.
- **Low-bandwidth or rural learners:** installable app, works without AI.
- **Mock-test analysis:** why marks were lost (error types, negative marking).
- **Personal notes:** turn class notes into explanations and practice.

## 10. For every age
| Group | How PrepPilot fits |
|---|---|
| Class 1–5 | Short sessions, easy-first questions, buddy encouragement, enforced breaks |
| Class 6–10 | Habit building (streaks, daily goal), mistake review, chapter map |
| Class 11–12 (boards) | Chapter readiness, exam countdown plan, notes-to-quiz |
| JEE / NEET aspirants | Negative-marking strategy, predicted score, official past questions, time-pressure diagnosis |
| Droppers and adult learners | Bring your own notes and papers, AI tutor, flexible pacing |

## 11. How teachers can use it
- **Adaptive homework:** upload their own question paper or worksheet as an adaptive quiz for the class.
- **Assign chapters:** students practise them adaptively.
- **See why students fail:** concept gaps vs careless slips vs traps, in place of plain scores.
- **Spaced review:** brings back what students got wrong, so topics don't fade.
- **Catch faulty keys:** the AI question check flags wrong answer keys in their papers.
- **Roadmap:** a class dashboard (common misconceptions per chapter), parent read-only reports.

## 12. Unique selling points (USPs)
1. **Explainable adaptation:** every question comes with *why*, plus a live graph.
2. **Diagnoses how you fail**, not just how often.
3. **Exam strategy coaching** for negative marking and confidence.
4. **Wellbeing built in:** enforced breaks, fatigue detection, encouragement.
5. **Your own material:** papers and notes become adaptive practice.
6. **Honest content:** verified keys, credited sources, AI tagged and double-checked.
7. **Works without AI or a subscription:** low cost, installable on any phone.

## 13. Market (approximate; verify before presenting)
- **NEET-UG:** about 23–24 lakh registered candidates a year (approx.).
- **JEE Main:** about 12–14 lakh candidates a year (approx.).
- **CBSE Class 10 + 12:** about 38–40 lakh students a year (approx.).
- **Target segments:** JEE/NEET aspirants first, then board students (Class 9–12), then coaching centres and schools (B2B).
- **Why now:** AI tutoring is mainstream, and installable web apps reach low-end phones without app stores.

## 14. Business model and go-to-market (proposed)
- **Freemium:** free core adaptive practice, verified bank and mistake review.
- **Pro (proposed, e.g. ₹99–149/month):** unlimited AI tutor, notes-to-quiz, advanced exam plan, detailed analytics.
- **B2B licences:** coaching centres and schools, per-student pricing, teacher tools and their own question banks.
- **Go-to-market:**
  1. pilot with 2–3 coaching centres or schools and measure learning gains;
  2. student ambassadors at schools and JEE/NEET communities;
  3. content partnerships for larger verified question banks;
  4. Indian-language versions;
  5. referral streak rewards.

## 15. Honest limits and roadmap
- **Today:** small verified bank (154), no video lessons, no real-student study yet, single-server prototype.
- **Next:**
  - a larger verified bank;
  - a teacher class dashboard;
  - prerequisite-aware learning paths;
  - Indian languages;
  - a pilot study to measure learning gains;
  - hosted deployment with a fixed address.

================================================================================
END OF FILE: docs/PITCH_FACTS.md
================================================================================

# Roadmap

This list describes proposed work, not shipped functionality.

## Next milestone: dependable evaluation

- Add independent educator review of conceptual keys, imported mappings and generated content.
- Expand verified questions in the most-used chapters; display coverage honestly.
- Add repeatable browser tests for setup, dashboard, quiz, reporting and timer flows.
- Review keyboard navigation, contrast and screen-reader support.
- Test provider failures, long waits and recovery without losing the current answer.

## Before public deployment

- Replace local JSON and in-memory progress with transactional, account-linked storage.
- Authorize quiz requests and tie session ownership to authenticated users.
- Add rate limiting, token expiry, input limits and usage controls.
- Define retention, deletion, consent and a privacy policy for student data and AI calls.
- Establish migrations, backup/recovery and multi-worker behavior.

## Learning quality

- Evaluate adaptive vs static practice with real learners and agreed outcomes.
- Calibrate question difficulty and confidence estimates against collected evidence.
- Model actual exam section rules and chapter weights.
- Consider spaced repetition, prerequisites and goal/exam-date planning after core validation.

## Deferred

Peer benchmarking, community features, subscription systems and teacher dashboards
are not part of the current prototype.

# PrepPilot stages 4–6 implementation plan

## Goal Capsule
Finish the user's staged polish and feature request without new runtime libraries,
publishing, silent provider changes, or loss of learner data. Commit each stage
only after all AGENTS checks and 375/1440 light/dark page checks pass.

## Product Contract
Stage 4 adds purposeful motion, with reduced-motion opt-out and preserved selectors.
Stage 5 adds owned daily activity, goals, heatmap, optional exam planning, speech
and downloadable summary images. Stage 6 adds explicit fallback and abuse bounds,
removes only expired authentication/abandoned guest records, and documents deployment.
The source request's detailed item lists are acceptance requirements.

## Planning Contract
Reuse vanilla JavaScript, existing CSS tokens, SQLite and the pure engine. No new
runtime dependencies. Use existing `session_locked`, preferences, reporting and
provider routing. Motion uses transform/opacity, at most 400 ms except breathing;
the requested 600 ms moon transition is capped at 400 ms by the global constraint.
Daily activity uses server UTC calendar days, labelled in UI; answering/reviewing
counts once per unique answer event, never a page visit. Goal defaults to 20.
An exam date is optional; pure planning returns suggestions, never automatic quizzes.
Fallback remains off unless saved explicitly; the reply names the actual provider.
One server worker remains required for in-memory coordination and request limits.
Guest cleanup uses last authenticated activity, includes token activity, and never
deletes account-owned records. Migrate existing guest identities conservatively.

## Implementation Units
### Stage 4: motion
Use CSS/shared DOM hooks where possible; preserve IDs and keyboard interactions.
Target index.html, galaxy.js, study.html, progress.html, review.html, pwa.js/site.css.
Add why cross-fade/chapter pulse, explicit moon navigation after 400 ms, view
transitions, breathing circle, clock ring, card stagger, progress bars/counter,
review result flip and mastered celebration. Test reduced motion and no duplicate submits.

### Stage 5: learner tools
Add SQLite daily activity table and goal/date preferences. Record activity on answer
and review, return `/api/daily`, derive streak with pure engine helper. Add pure
`plan_day()` using best gains/review count/goal, plus asserts. Render hub activity,
heatmap and plan with textContent. Add speech controls only when supported and
summary canvas export. API tests cover isolation, duplicates, dates, invalid input,
restart persistence, streak gaps and planning edge cases.

### Stage 6: reliability
Wrap AI routing with one explicit opt-in fallback, preserve JSON/text return contracts
and surface actual-provider notices. Use bounded sliding request windows per IP
for auth and per owner for AI; return helpful 429/Retry-After. Startup cleanup removes
expired tokens and guest-owned data inactive 30 days. Test expiry boundaries,
authenticated active guests, account retention, rate expiry and disabled fallback.
Document single-worker deployment, HTTPS cookies, environment and Google origins.

## Verification Contract
Run every AGENTS command at each stage. Test touched pages at 375/1440 light/dark
using isolated DB and offline providers, and reduced-motion interaction separately.
Keep browser/source safety and ownership checks. No keys or learner data in commits.

## Definition of Done
Each requested stage has its own local commit and observed passing checks, or a
documented rollback of that stage. Update README/HANDOFF and report commit IDs,
test outcomes and required manual deployment/configuration steps. No push.

================================================================================
END OF FILE: docs/STAGED_IMPLEMENTATION.md

# GitHub and hackathon submission copy

## Suggested repository name

`preppilot`

## GitHub About description

Adaptive study companion with explainable question selection, confidence-aware feedback, chapter quizzes and optional Gemini tutoring. Early hackathon prototype built with FastAPI and vanilla JavaScript.

## Suggested topics

`hackathon`, `adaptive-learning`, `edtech`, `fastapi`, `python`, `quiz`, `gemini`, `jee`, `neet`

## One-line pitch

PrepPilot adapts to how a learner struggles and explains why it chooses the next question.

## Short abstract

PrepPilot helps learners practise at a difficulty suited to their current understanding.
After choosing a class, starting difficulty and focus time, learners browse subjects
and chapters and begin an adaptive quiz. A Python learner model uses correctness,
confidence, timing and hints to update chapter-level ability and select follow-up
questions. A reasoning panel makes these choices visible. Optional Gemini calls
provide generated questions, explanations and doubt support, while a checked-in
154-item bank supports selected chapters without an AI key. The current milestone
is a working local prototype with automated checks; real learner validation and
permanent account-linked progress are planned.

## What we built

- Setup and a responsive subject/chapter study dashboard.
- Explainable adaptive quiz selection with response-pattern feedback.
- A checked question bank and optional AI-assisted content.
- Focus blocks, breaks, reporting and same-server session resume.

## Honest early-evaluation status

The prototype works locally. Eight API regression tests, the engine self-check and
bank checks pass. A synthetic simulation illustrates the adaptive policy; it does
not establish improved outcomes in real learners. Progress is in memory, quiz APIs
are unauthenticated, and bank coverage is limited. This is not a deployed service.

## Before uploading

- Fill in actual team names, hackathon details and demo/video links in the portal.
- Use `QUIZBOT-github-ready.zip` as the clean source upload, not the private working folder.
- Keep the repository unlicensed as requested; third-party notices still apply.
- Confirm README screenshots represent the submitted build.
- Run the documented checks; confirm the GitHub workflow passes after upload.
- Do not upload `users.json`, keys, local environments or the `session/` transcript.

The existing local Git history contains `session/claude-session-transcript.zip`.
Adding an ignore rule does not remove a tracked file or its history. The clean ZIP
contains no `.git` directory or transcript; initialize a fresh repository from its
contents if publishing that package. Do not push the old history without reviewing it.

# Third-party notices

## JEEBench

`preppilot/questions_imported.json` contains 74 single-correct MCQs imported from
[JEEBench](https://github.com/dair-iitd/jeebench), which contains JEE Advanced
2016–2023 problems. `import_jeebench.py` transforms and maps these records.
The upstream repository is MIT-licensed. Its license and copyright notice are
preserved verbatim in [JEEBENCH_LICENSE.txt](JEEBENCH_LICENSE.txt); that notice does
not license PrepPilot's original code. Source labels and official-key provenance
are retained in the imported records. No independent rights audit of original
exam content has been performed.

Reference: Daman Arora, Himanshu Singh and Mausam (2023),
[Have LLMs Advanced Enough? A Challenging Problem Solving Benchmark For Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.468/),
EMNLP 2023, pages 7527–7543. DOI: 10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.468.

## Runtime and browser dependencies

FastAPI, Uvicorn and the test dependency httpx are installed using pip; their
transitive dependencies retain their own licenses. KaTeX and Chart.js are loaded
from CDNs, and Google Fonts serves Bricolage Grotesque and Instrument Sans.
Those packages/font files retain their respective upstream licenses.
Gemini is an optional hosted service subject to the provider's terms.

## Visual references and original assets

The study dashboard was inspired by user-supplied learning-platform screenshots.
No Physics Wallah logo, screenshot, course artwork or branded assets are bundled
as product assets. Screenshots in docs/assets show the PrepPilot prototype.

FILE: SECURITY.md

# Security and privacy status

PrepPilot is a local hackathon prototype. Accounts and guest identities now protect
learner sessions, preferences and owned papers. They do not make it production-ready.

## Implemented

- Salted PBKDF2-SHA256 password hashes; SQLite account uniqueness and transactions.
- Random 30-day authentication tokens stored only as SHA-256 digests in SQLite.
- HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax authentication cookies; logout revokes the current token.
- Ownership checks for learner data and papers, including resumed session IDs.
- Cross-origin mutation requests with a mismatched Origin header are rejected.
- Frontend display caches do not establish identity. API keys stay on the server.

`PREPPILOT_SECURE_COOKIES=1` enables Secure cookies for HTTPS deployments. Local HTTP
uses non-Secure cookies for development. One worker remains required because learner
objects and mutation locks are cached in-process. No new public deployment is implied.

## Private data

`preppilot.sqlite3` and its WAL/SHM files contain account and learning data. They are
Git-ignored, as are old account/paper files and `.env.local`. Back up the database using
the migration guide. Old unowned papers are not silently assigned or served to accounts.
Guest progress is accessible through the guest cookie and cannot be recovered through
an account if that cookie is lost or expires.

Selected Gemini/Groq requests can include question context, chosen answers and doubt
text. Avoid real student personal information in evaluation. Scripts and fonts may
load from CDNs. The provider selector never exposes API keys.

## Remaining work

Rate limiting, account recovery, a retention/deletion policy, administrator controls,
comprehensive security auditing and public-service privacy documentation remain
unfinished. Public catalog/simulation routes are not a production abuse boundary.

## Reporting

Do not post keys, account databases or exploit details in public issues. Use private
vulnerability reporting if enabled, or arrange a private channel with the repository
owner. No response SLA or monitored security address is claimed.

================================================================================
END OF FILE: SECURITY.md

# Contributing

This is an early hackathon prototype and no project-wide license has been selected.
Discuss nontrivial contributions with the maintainers before submitting work.

## Development

Follow the root README to create an environment and install
`preppilot/requirements-dev.txt`. Run the engine, bank and regression checks from
`preppilot/` before proposing changes. Keep the server single-worker.

- Keep learner policy pure in `engine.py`; add a relevant self-check for policy changes.
- Add bank checks for original questions and preserve source attribution for imports.
- Use textContent for question, AI and learner text; do not inject it through innerHTML.
- Preserve responsive layouts and both colour schemes.
- Do not commit accounts, API keys, private transcripts or installed dependencies.
- Explain the observed problem, resulting behavior and validation in your PR.

The current eight regression tests are in `preppilot/test_regressions.py`.
Changes to the browser flow also require a browser check; Python tests alone do not
validate appearance or frontend interactions. Do not add unsupported learning claims.

**Repository name:** `preppilot`

Adaptive AI study companion for Class 1–12, JEE and NEET.
